        <p>(e) State park managers must where practicable recycle the gathered recyclable materials, provide for the local unit of government to recycle the gathered materials, or contract with private nonprofit groups for recycling.</p>
        <p>(f) Money collected by state park managers for recycling must be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the general fund.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.205.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/205/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 205 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/594/#laws.3.3.0">1990 c 594 art 3 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.21">
        <h1 class="shn">85.21 STATE OPERATION OF PARK, MONUMENT, RECREATION AREA AND WAYSIDE FACILITIES; LICENSE NOT REQUIRED.</h1>
        <p>The state, in its operation of state park, state monument, state recreation area, and state wayside facilities, shall not be required to obtain or pay for permits or licenses required by statute or by ordinance of governmental subdivisions of private operators in conducting or maintaining similar business or facilities.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.21.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1945/0/370/#laws.0.1.0">1945 c 370 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/525/#laws.0.5.0">1969 c 525 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.22">
        <h1 class="shn">85.22 STATE PARKS WORKING CAPITAL ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.22.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Designation.</span></h2>
          <p>The revolving fund established under Laws 1941, chapter 548, section 37, subdivision E, item 4 is the state parks working capital account. The account is to be used to maintain and operate the revenue producing facilities and to operate the resource management and interpretive programs in the state parks within the limits in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.22.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/901/#laws.0.72.0">1965 c 901 s 72</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.22.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Receipts, appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>All receipts derived from the rental or sale of state park items, tours, interpretation programs, educational programs, and operation of Douglas Lodge shall be deposited in the state treasury and be credited to the state parks working capital account. Receipts and expenses from Douglas Lodge shall be tracked separately within the account. Money in the account is annually appropriated for the purchase and payment of expenses attributable to items for resale or rental and operation of Douglas Lodge. Any excess receipts in this account are annually appropriated for state park management and interpretive programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.22.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Charges sufficient to defray expenses.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1283">16A.1283</a>, the commissioner of natural resources shall, by written order, adjust the schedule of charges for operating facilities within state parks so as to produce income sufficient to defray all expenses required to provide proper operations of said facilities. An adjustment to the schedule of charges is not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.22.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/901/#laws.0.72.0">1965 c 901 s 72</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.22.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Exemption.</span></h2>
          <p>Purchases for resale or rental made from the state parks working capital account are exempt from competitive bidding, notwithstanding chapter 16C.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.22.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1951/0/460/#laws.0.1.0">1951 c 460 s 1</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/901/#laws.0.71.0">1965 c 901 s 71</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/399/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 399 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.276.0">1981 c 356 s 276</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.199.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 199</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.23.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 23</a>,24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/254/#laws.2.17.0">1991 c 254 art 2 s 17</a>,18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/172/#laws.0.37.0">1993 c 172 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/28/#laws.1.10.0">2003 c 28 art 1 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/221/#laws.0.21.0">2004 c 221 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.17.0">2004 c 255 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/10/#laws.1.21.0">2005 c 10 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.31.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.35.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 35</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.23">
        <h1 class="shn">85.23 COOPERATIVE LEASES OF AGRICULTURAL LANDS.</h1>
        <p>(a) On any agricultural lands however acquired, located within a state park, where the use of the lands would not conflict with normal park uses, cooperative farming leases on a sharecrop basis may be completed with nearby farmers to provide needed park services such as grassland restoration or development, weed control, and the provision of food plots, such as corn, for deer, pheasants and other wildlife species that are indigenous to the habitats existing in state parks and which are of value and interest to state park visitors and the general public.</p>
        <p>(b) The sharecropping leases may be entered into for state park lands in lieu of any rental arrangement established for use with respect to state lands in any other provision of law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.23.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/249/#laws.0.1.0">1977 c 249 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.85.26"><b>85.26</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.85.27"><b>85.27</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.85.28"><b>85.28</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.85.29"><b>85.29</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.85.30"><b>85.30</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/594/#laws.3.15.0">1990 c 594 art 3 s 15</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.85.31"><b>85.31</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.32">
        <h1 class="shn">85.32 STATE WATER TRAILS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.32.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Designation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of natural resources is authorized in cooperation with local units of government and private individuals and groups when feasible to manage state water trails on the Lake Superior water trail under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.0155">85.0155</a> and on the following rivers, which have historic, recreational, and scenic values: Little Fork, Big Fork, Minnesota, St. Croix, Snake, Mississippi, Red Lake, Cannon, Straight, Des Moines, Crow Wing, St. Louis, Pine, Rum, Kettle, Cloquet, Root, Zumbro, Pomme de Terre within Swift County, Watonwan, Cottonwood, Whitewater, Chippewa from Benson in Swift County to Montevideo in Chippewa County, Long Prairie, Red River of the North, Sauk, Otter Tail, Redwood, Blue Earth, Cedar, Shell Rock, Vermilion in St. Louis County, north fork of the Crow, and south fork of the Crow. The commissioner may map and sign points of interest, public water access sites, portages, camp sites, and dams, rapids, waterfalls, and other serious hazards that are dangerous to canoe, kayak, and watercraft travelers. The commissioner may maintain passageway for watercraft on state water trails.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10.49">10.49</a>, the segment of the St. Croix River Water Trail between Wild River State Park and William O'Brien State Park is designated and named the Walter F. Mondale Scenic Riverway.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.32.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Camp sites, rest areas, river accesses and portages; land acquisition.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may, in cooperation with local units of government as provided in subdivision 1, acquire land by purchase, grant, gift, devise, exchange, lease or easement for camp sites, rest areas, river accesses and portages. The acquisition of any parcel of land pursuant to this subdivision shall not exceed 20 acres, unless it will or should require a land survey or a larger parcel will be in the best interest of the state, in which case the commissioner may acquire land not to exceed 40 acres or unless specifically authorized by the executive council. The commissioner may also develop and be responsible for the maintenance of campsites, rest areas, river accesses, and portages on the areas acquired, or in cooperation with local units of government, organizations, or individuals, along those rivers designated in subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.32.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Dedication; application of statute.</span></h2>
          <p>Areas acquired by easement or lease or areas designated and marked under this section shall not be subject to the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/160.06">160.06</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.32.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1963/0/386/#laws.0.1.0">1963 c 386 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/862/#laws.0.1.0">1967 c 862 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/905/#laws.0.5.0">1967 c 905 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/37/#laws.0.1.0">1975 c 37 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/24/#laws.0.1.0">1976 c 24 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/346/#laws.0.3.0">1976 c 346 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/224/#laws.0.1.0">1977 c 224 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/116/#laws.0.1.0">1983 c 116 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/220/#laws.0.69.0">1995 c 220 s 69</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/280/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 280 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/381/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 381 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.88.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 88</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.1.17.0">2006 c 281 art 1 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.9.7.0">2006 c 282 art 9 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.42.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.32.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.39.0">2011 c 107 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.38.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.36.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.30.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 30</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.33">
        <h1 class="shn">85.33 ST. CROIX WILD RIVER AREA; LIMITATIONS ON POWER BOATING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.33.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, "motorboat" means any contrivance used or designed for navigation on water which is propelled in any respect by machinery, including but not limited to detachable motors.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.33.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Purpose; rules above mouth of Snake River.</span></h2>
          <p>In furtherance of the public policy declared by Congress in Public Law 90-542 and Public Law 92-560 of maintaining the essentially wild and scenic character of certain rivers, including a part of the St. Croix, and as an exercise of state powers either recognized or granted by Public Law 90-542 and Public Law 92-560, the commissioner of natural resources may promulgate rules, in the manner provided by chapter 14, which limit or prohibit the operation or use of motorboats on that part of the St. Croix River north of the mouth of the Snake River, located in Section 31, Township 39 North, Range 19 West.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.33.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Rules below mouth of Snake River.</span></h2>
          <p>After October 1, 1974, if the commissioner of natural resources has not established rules relating to the use of watercraft on that part of the St. Croix River south of the mouth of the Snake River but north of the nine-foot navigational channel at mile 24.5, measured from the mouth of the St. Croix River, pursuant to the request of a local governmental unit in the manner provided by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86B.205#stat.86B.205.9">86B.205, subdivision 9</a>, the commissioner may establish such rules pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86B.205#stat.86B.205.9">86B.205, subdivision 9</a>, notwithstanding the absence of a request from a local governmental unit and notwithstanding the absence of approval of the rules by a majority of the counties affected.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.33.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Concurrent rules required.</span></h2>
          <p>Rules promulgated by the commissioner pursuant to this section are effective upon adoption of laws or rules providing for similar limitations or prohibitions on the operation and use of motorboats on the same reach of St. Croix River by the state of Wisconsin or its agencies. In exercising the authority granted by this section the commissioner may enter into necessary agreements with either the United States and its agencies, pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.025">84.025</a>, or the state of Wisconsin and its agencies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.33.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>A person violating any of the provisions of this section or the rules promulgated thereunder by the commissioner is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.33.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/557/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 557 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.8.16.0">1990 c 391 art 8 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.34">
        <h1 class="shn">85.34 FORT SNELLING LEASE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.34.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Upper bluff; lease terms.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources with the approval of the Executive Council may lease for purposes of restoration, preservation, historical, recreational, educational, and commercial use and development, that portion of Fort Snelling State Park known as the upper bluff consisting of officer's row, area J, the polo grounds, the adjacent golf course, and all buildings and improvements located thereon, all lying within an area bounded by Minneapolis-St. Paul International Airport, Trunk Highways numbered 5 and 55, and Bloomington Road. The lease or leases shall be in a form approved by the attorney general and for a term of not to exceed 99 years. The lease or leases may provide for the provision of capital improvements or other performance by the tenant or tenants in lieu of all or some of the payments of rent that would otherwise be required. Notwithstanding the continuing ownership of the upper bluff by the state, any lease of one or more buildings improved with state general obligation bond proceeds that exceeds 50 years shall be treated as a sale of the buildings for purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.695">16A.695</a>, subdivision 3. Any disposition proceeds payable to the commissioner upon execution of a lease relating to state-bond-financed buildings at the upper bluff shall be applied according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.695#stat.16A.695.3">16A.695, subdivision 3</a>, and used to pay, redeem, or defease state general obligation bonds issued for purposes of improving those buildings. Any lease revenues paid to the commissioner subsequent to the payment, redemption, or defeasance of state general obligation bonds shall be used by the commissioner as further described in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.34.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Admission.</span></h2>
          <p>Admission to the property leased pursuant to subdivision 1 shall be exempt from any state park permit or admission fees imposed pursuant to law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.34.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Sale of intoxicating liquor.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of public safety with the approval of the Executive Council may issue to the lessee or developer of the property leased pursuant to subdivision 1, an on-sale license for the sale of intoxicating liquor upon the leased property. The annual fee for the license issued pursuant to this subdivision shall be set by the commissioner of public safety at an amount comparable to the fee charged by municipalities in the surrounding area for a similar license. All provisions of chapter 340A shall apply to the sale of intoxicating liquor upon the leased property.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.34.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.51.0">2004 c 255 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.34.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Rule exception.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources may provide an exception, in whole or in part, to the rules for use of state parks and other recreational areas for property leased pursuant to subdivision 1. The exception may be provided by commissioner's order and shall be effective for the term of the lease or such lesser period of time specified by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.34.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Minneapolis lease.</span></h2>
          <p>A lease to the Minneapolis Park and Recreation Board for the purposes of athletic fields and golf course operations is subject to subdivisions 1 to 5, except as provided in this subdivision. Approval of the Executive Council is not required for the lease or the issuance of a liquor license. A lease of any portion of Officer's Row or Area J may include a charge to be paid by the tenant for repayment of a portion of the costs incurred by the Minneapolis Park and Recreation Board for the installation of a new water line on the upper bluff. The total amount to be repaid to the Minneapolis Park and Recreation Board by tenants of Officer's Row and Area J shall not exceed $450,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.34.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Disposition of proceeds.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) All revenue derived from the lease of the Fort Snelling upper bluff, with the exception of payment for costs of the water line as described in subdivision 6, shall be deposited in the natural resources fund and credited to a state park account. Interest earned on the money in the account accrues to the account.</p>
          <p>(b) Revenue and expenses from the upper bluff shall be tracked separately within the account. Money in the account derived from the leasing or operation of the property described in subdivision 1 is appropriated annually to the commissioner for the payment of expenses attributable to the leasing, development, and operation of the property described in subdivision 1, including, but not limited to, the maintenance, repair, and rehabilitation of historic buildings and landscapes.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.34.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/573/#laws.0.1.0">1978 c 573 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/488/#laws.3.23.0">2000 c 488 art 3 s 23</a>-25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.18.0">2004 c 255 s 18</a>,19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.13.16.0">2014 c 312 art 13 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/8/#laws.2.5.0">1Sp2017 c 8 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.12.0">2023 c 25 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">TRAIL PASSES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.40">
        <h1 class="shn">85.40 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.40.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.40">85.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.45">85.45</a> the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.40.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of natural resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.40.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Cross-country ski grant-in-aid program.</span></h2>
          <p>"Cross-country ski grant-in-aid program" means a program administered by the department, as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.44">85.44</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.40.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Cross-country-ski race.</span></h2>
          <p>"Cross-country-ski race" means a timed skiing event organized for the participation of a large number of skiers at one time over a course prepared specifically for a ski race.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.40.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Cross-country-ski trail.</span></h2>
          <p>"Cross-country-ski trail" means a public pathway designated and promoted for cross-country skiing in state parks as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.012">85.012</a>, on state forest lands as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/89.001">89.001</a>, on state trails as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.015">85.015</a>, on elements of the regional recreation open space system as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.147">473.147</a>, or on trails within the cross-country ski grant-in-aid program as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.44">85.44</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.40.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Cross-country skiing.</span></h2>
          <p>"Cross-country skiing" means traveling across country over snow by human power on skis. "Cross-country skiing" does not require the use of lifts, tows, or other mechanical devices.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.40.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Department.</span></h2>
          <p>"Department" means the Department of Natural Resources.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.40.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/325/#laws.0.2.0">1983 c 325 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.86.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 86</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.105.0">1999 c 231 s 105</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.41">
        <h1 class="shn">85.41 CROSS-COUNTRY-SKI PASSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.41.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Pass in possession.</span></h2>
          <p>While skiing on cross-country-ski trails, a person age 16 or over shall carry in immediate possession a valid cross-country-ski pass. A landowner who grants an easement for a grant-in-aid ski trail is not required to have a pass when skiing on the landowner's property.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.41.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">License agents.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may appoint agents to issue and sell cross-country-ski passes. The commissioner may revoke the appointment of an agent at any time.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may promulgate additional rules as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.485#stat.97A.485.11">97A.485, subdivision 11</a>. An agent shall observe all rules promulgated by the commissioner for the accounting and handling of licenses pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.485#stat.97A.485.11">97A.485, subdivision 11</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) An agent must promptly deposit and remit all moneys received from the sale of passes, except issuing fees, to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.41.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Participants in cross-country-ski races and residents of a state or local government operated correctional facility are exempt from the pass requirement in subdivision 1 if a special use permit has been obtained by the organizers of the event or those in an official capacity in advance from the agency with jurisdiction over the cross-country-ski trail. Permits shall require that permit holders return the trail and any associated facility to its original condition if any damage is done by the permittee. Limited permits for special events may be issued and shall require the removal of any trail markers, banners, and other material used in connection with the special event.</p>
          <p>(b) Unless otherwise exempted under paragraph (a), students, teachers, and supervising adults engaged in school-sanctioned activities or youth activities sponsored by a nonprofit organization are exempt from the pass requirements in subdivision 1.</p>
          <p>(c) A resident that is in the armed forces of the United States, stationed outside of the state, and in the state on leave is exempt from the pass requirement in subdivision 1 if the resident possesses official military leave papers.</p>
          <p>(d) A resident who has served at any time during the preceding 24 months in federal active service, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/190.05">190.05</a>, subdivision 5c, outside the United States as a member of the National Guard, or as a reserve component or active duty member of the United Stated armed forces and has been discharged from active service is exempt from the pass requirement in subdivision 1 if the resident possesses official military discharge papers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.41.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Issuance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner and agents shall issue and sell cross-country-ski passes. The pass shall be with the skier and available for inspection by any peace or conservation officer. The pass shall include the applicant's information deemed necessary by the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.41.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Issuing fee.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to the fee for a cross-country-ski pass, an issuing fee of $1 per pass shall be charged. The issuing fee shall be retained by the seller of the pass. Issuing fees for passes issued by the commissioner shall be deposited in the cross-country-ski account in the natural resources fund and retained for the operation of the electronic licensing system. A pass shall indicate the amount of the fee that is retained by the seller.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.41.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/325/#laws.0.3.0">1983 c 325 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.87.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 87</a>-89; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.2.6.0">1987 c 149 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.101.0">1987 c 404 s 101</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 231 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.106.0">1999 c 231 s 106</a>-108; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/260/#laws.0.14.0">2000 c 260 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.20.0">2004 c 255 s 20</a>-22; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.33.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.14.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 14</a>,15</p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendments to subdivisions 1 and 4 by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, sections 14 and 15, are effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.42">
        <h1 class="shn">85.42 USER FEE; VALIDITY.</h1>
        <p>(a) The fee for an annual cross-country-ski pass is $24 for an individual age 16 and over. The fee for a three-year pass is $69 for an individual age 16 and over. This fee must be collected at the time the pass is purchased. Three-year passes are valid for three years beginning the previous July 1. Annual passes are valid for one year beginning the previous July 1.</p>
        <p>(b) The cost for a daily cross-country skier pass is $9 for an individual age 16 and over. This fee must be collected at the time the pass is purchased. The daily pass is valid only for the date designated on the pass form.</p>
        <p>(c) A pass must be signed by the skier across the front of the pass to be valid and is nontransferable when signed.</p>
        <p>(d) The commissioner and agents must issue a duplicate pass to a person whose pass is lost or destroyed using the process established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.405#stat.97A.405.3">97A.405, subdivision 3</a>, and rules adopted thereunder. The fee for a duplicate cross-country-ski pass is $2.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.42.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/325/#laws.0.4.0">1983 c 325 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.102.0">1987 c 404 s 102</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.109.0">1999 c 231 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.56.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.34.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.20.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.31.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.43">
        <h1 class="shn">85.43 DISPOSITION OF RECEIPTS; PURPOSE.</h1>
        <p> Fees from cross-country-ski passes shall be deposited in the state treasury and credited to a cross-country-ski account in the natural resources fund and, except for the electronic licensing system commission established by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027">84.027</a>, subdivision 15, are appropriated to the commissioner of natural resources for:</p>
        <p>(1) grants-in-aid for cross-country-ski trails to:</p>
        <p>(i) counties and municipalities for construction and maintenance of cross-country-ski trails; and</p>
        <p>(ii) special park districts as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.44">85.44</a> for construction and maintenance of cross-country-ski trails;</p>
        <p>(2) administering the cross-country-ski trail grant-in-aid program; and</p>
        <p>(3) developing and maintaining state cross-country-ski trails.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.43.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/325/#laws.0.5.0">1983 c 325 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.200.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 200</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.103.0">1987 c 404 s 103</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.23.0">2004 c 255 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.57.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.35.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.40.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 40</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.44">
        <h1 class="shn">85.44 CROSS-COUNTRY-SKI TRAIL GRANT-IN-AID PROGRAM.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall establish a grant-in-aid program for local units of government and special park districts to acquire, develop, and maintain cross-country-ski trails that are determined by the commissioner to be part of the state's grant-in-aid system. Grants are available to acquire trail easements but may not be used to acquire any lands in fee title. Local units of government and special park districts applying for and receiving grants under this section are considered to have cross-country-ski trails for one year following the expiration of their last grant. The department shall reimburse all public sponsors of grants-in-aid cross-country-ski trails based upon criteria established by the department. Before using any reimbursement criteria, a certain proportion of the revenues must be allocated on the basis of user fee sales location. The commissioner may establish a performance-based funding formula for annual grants-in-aid. The procedures and criteria for grants-in-aid are not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply. In administering the performance-based grants-in-aid, the commissioner must:</p>
        <p>(1) determine annual grant amounts based on a funding formula that includes consideration of historical costs, snowfall, use, and tourism;</p>
        <p>(2) make grant payments based on:</p>
        <p>(i) successful completion of performance benchmarks;</p>
        <p>(ii) reimbursement of eligible expenditures; or</p>
        <p>(iii) a combination of items (i) and (ii); and</p>
        <p>(3) assess penalties to nonperforming grant-in-aid recipients, which may include withholding grant payments or making the grantee or trail system ineligible for future grant-in-aid funding.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.44.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/325/#laws.0.6.0">1983 c 325 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.110.0">1999 c 231 s 110</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.32.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 32</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.45">
        <h1 class="shn">85.45 PENALTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.45.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Skiing without pass.</span></h2>
          <p>No person may ski on a cross-country-ski trail without a valid cross-country-ski pass. Any person who violates this subdivision is guilty of a petty misdemeanor.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.45.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">False statements.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who knowingly makes a false statement related to an application for a cross-country-ski pass is guilty of a petty misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.45.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/325/#laws.0.7.0">1983 c 325 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.104.0">1987 c 404 s 104</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.4.0">1993 c 231 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.111.0">1999 c 231 s 111</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.16.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 1 by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 16, is effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.46">
        <h1 class="shn">85.46 HORSE PASS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.46.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Pass in possession.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), while riding, leading, or driving a horse on lands administered by the commissioner, except forest roads and forest roads rights-of-way, a person 16 years of age or over shall carry in immediate possession a valid horse pass. The pass must be available for inspection by a peace officer, a conservation officer, or an employee designated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0835">84.0835</a>. A person who violates any provision of this subdivision is guilty of a petty misdemeanor.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A valid horse pass is not required under this section for a person riding, leading, or driving a horse on property that is owned by the person or the person's spouse, child, parent, or guardian.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.46.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">License agents.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of natural resources may appoint agents to issue and sell horse passes. The commissioner may revoke the appointment of an agent at any time.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may adopt additional rules as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.485">97A.485</a>, subdivision 11. An agent shall observe all rules adopted by the commissioner for the accounting and handling of passes according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.485">97A.485</a>, subdivision 11.</p>
          <p>(c) An agent must promptly deposit and remit all money received from the sale of passes, except issuing fees, to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.46.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Issuance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources and agents shall issue and sell horse passes. The pass shall include the applicant's information deemed necessary by the commissioner. To be valid, a daily or annual pass must be issued to the person riding, leading, or driving the horse, and a commercial annual pass must be issued to the owner of the commercial riding facility.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.46.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Pass fees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The fee for an annual horse pass is $20 for an individual 16 years of age and over. The fee shall be collected at the time the pass is purchased. Annual passes are valid for one year beginning January 1 and ending December 31.</p>
          <p>(b) The fee for a daily horse pass is $4 for an individual 16 years of age and over. The fee shall be collected at the time the pass is purchased. The daily pass is valid only for the date designated on the pass form.</p>
          <p>(c) The fee for a commercial annual horse pass is $200 and includes issuance of 15 passes. Additional or individual commercial annual horse passes may be purchased by the commercial riding facility owner at a fee of $20 each. Commercial annual horse passes are valid for one year beginning January 1 and ending December 31 and may be affixed to the horse tack, saddle, or person. Commercial annual horse passes are not transferable to another commercial riding facility. For the purposes of this section, a "commercial riding facility" is an operation where horses are used for riding instruction or other equestrian activities for hire or use by others.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.46.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Issuing fee.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to the fee for a horse pass, an issuing fee of $1 per pass shall be charged. The issuing fee shall be retained by the seller of the pass. Issuing fees for passes sold by the commissioner of natural resources shall be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the horse pass account in the natural resources fund and are appropriated to the commissioner for the operation of the electronic licensing system. A pass shall indicate the amount of the fee that is retained by the seller.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.46.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Disposition of receipts.</span></h2>
          <p>Fees collected under this section, except for the issuing fee, shall be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the horse pass account in the natural resources fund. Except for the electronic licensing system commission established by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027">84.027</a>, subdivision 15, the fees are appropriated to the commissioner of natural resources for trail acquisition, trail and facility development, and maintenance, enforcement, and rehabilitation of horse trails or trails authorized for horse use, whether for riding, leading, or driving, on land administered by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.46.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Duplicate horse passes.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources and agents shall issue a duplicate pass to a person or commercial riding facility owner whose pass is lost or destroyed using the process established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.405">97A.405</a>, subdivision 3, and rules adopted thereunder. The fee for a duplicate horse pass is $2, with an issuing fee of 50 cents.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.46.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.9.8.0">2006 c 282 art 9 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.2.7.0">2008 c 368 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.22.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 22</a>-24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.36.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.20.0">2012 c 272 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.17.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 17</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 3 by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 17, is effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.47">
        <h1 class="shn">85.47 SPECIAL-USE PERMITS; FEES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.47.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Special-use permits.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may, by written order, develop reasonable policies for special-use permits to use state trails and state water access sites. The policies are exempt from the rulemaking provisions under chapter 14, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.47.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Disposition of fees.</span></h2>
          <p>Fees collected for special-use permits to use state trails and state water access sites not on state forest, state park, or state recreation area lands must be deposited in the natural resources fund and are appropriated to the commissioner of natural resources for operating and maintaining state trails and water access sites.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.47.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.37.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.33.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.41.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">CENTRAL MINNESOTA REGIONAL PARKS AND TRAILS PLAN</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.50">
        <h1 class="shn">85.50 DEFINITION.</h1>
        <p>For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.50">85.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.52">85.52</a>, "central Minnesota regional parks and trails plan" or "plan" means a recreational park and trail plan in the counties of Stearns, Benton, and Sherburne that includes, at a minimum, existing regionally significant parks and trails that are identified in the plan under a local unit of government jurisdiction and includes newly developed parks and trails or trail connections to communities, parks, or public open space.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.50.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/65/#laws.0.1.0">1999 c 65 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.51">
        <h1 class="shn">85.51 PLANNING; COORDINATION.</h1>
        <p>Plan development, adoption, and amendments shall be coordinated with the counties of Stearns, Benton, and Sherburne by an appropriate regional parks and trail coordination board that is created under a joint powers agreement.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.51.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/65/#laws.0.2.0">1999 c 65 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.52">
        <h1 class="shn">85.52 GRANTS; PRIORITIES.</h1>
        <p>The Regional Parks and Trail Coordination Board under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.51">85.51</a> may administer grants from the United States, the state, or other sources to develop, enhance, or maintain the central Minnesota regional parks and trails plan, and must dispose of grant money in accordance with the acceptance of any agreement or contract. In developing, enhancing, and maintaining the plan, the Parks and Trails Coordination Board must develop priorities for expenditure of grant money.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.52.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/65/#laws.0.3.0">1999 c 65 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PARKS AND TRAILS FUND</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.53">
        <h1 class="shn">85.53 PARKS AND TRAILS FUND.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.53.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The parks and trails fund is established in the Minnesota Constitution, article XI, section 15. All money earned by the parks and trails fund must be credited to the fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.53.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Expenditures; accountability.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A project or program receiving funding from the parks and trails fund must meet or exceed the constitutional requirement to support parks and trails of regional or statewide significance. A project or program receiving funding from the parks and trails fund must include measurable outcomes, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.303">3.303</a>, subdivision 10; a plan for measuring and evaluating the results; and an assessment of whether the funding celebrates cultural diversity or reaches diverse communities in Minnesota, including reaching low- and moderate-income households. A project or program must be consistent with current science and incorporate state-of-the-art technology, except when the project or program is a portrayal or restoration of historical significance.</p>
          <p>(b) Money from the parks and trails fund shall be expended to balance the benefits across all regions and residents of the state.</p>
          <p>(c) A state agency or other recipient of a direct appropriation from the parks and trails fund must compile and submit all information for funded projects or programs, including the proposed measurable outcomes and all other items required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.303#stat.3.303.10">3.303, subdivision 10</a>, to the Legislative Coordinating Commission as soon as practicable or by January 15 of the applicable fiscal year, whichever comes first. The Legislative Coordinating Commission must post submitted information on the website required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.303">3.303</a>, subdivision 10, as soon as it becomes available.</p>
          <p>(d) Grants funded by the parks and trails fund must be implemented according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.98">16B.98</a> and must account for all expenditures. Proposals must specify a process for any regranting envisioned. Priority for grant proposals must be given to proposals involving grants that will be competitively awarded.</p>
          <p>(e) Money from the parks and trails fund may only be spent on projects located in Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(f) When practicable, a direct recipient of an appropriation from the parks and trails fund shall prominently display on the recipient's website home page the legacy logo required under Laws 2009, chapter 172, article 5, section 10, as amended by Laws 2010, chapter 361, article 3, section 5, accompanied by the phrase "Click here for more information." When a person clicks on the legacy logo image, the website must direct the person to a web page that includes both the contact information that a person may use to obtain additional information, as well as a link to the Legislative Coordinating Commission website required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.303#stat.3.303.10">3.303, subdivision 10</a>.</p>
          <p>(g) Future eligibility for money from the parks and trails fund is contingent upon a state agency or other recipient satisfying all applicable requirements in this section, as well as any additional requirements contained in applicable session law. If the Office of the Legislative Auditor, in the course of an audit or investigation, publicly reports that a recipient of money from the parks and trails fund has not complied with the laws, rules, or regulations in this section or other laws applicable to the recipient, the recipient must be listed in an annual report to the legislative committees with jurisdiction over the legacy funds. The list must be publicly available. The legislative auditor shall remove a recipient from the list upon determination that the recipient is in compliance. A recipient on the list is not eligible for future funding from the parks and trails fund until the recipient demonstrates compliance to the legislative auditor.</p>
          <p>(h) Any state agency or organization requesting a direct appropriation from the parks and trails fund must inform the house of representatives and senate committees having jurisdiction over the parks and trails fund, at the time the request for funding is made, whether the request is supplanting or is a substitution for any previous funding that was not from a legacy fund and was used for the same purpose.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.53.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Metropolitan area; distribution formula.</span></h2>
          <p>Money appropriated from the parks and trails fund to the Metropolitan Council shall be distributed to implementing agencies, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.351#stat.473.351.1">473.351, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a), as grants according to the following formula:</p>
          <p>(1) 45 percent of the money must be disbursed according to the allocation formula in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.351#stat.473.351.3">473.351, subdivision 3</a>, to each implementing agency;</p>
          <p>(2) 31.5 percent of the money must be distributed based on each implementing agency's relative share of the most recent estimate of the population of the metropolitan area;</p>
          <p>(3) 13.5 percent of the money must be distributed based on each implementing agency's relative share of nonlocal visits based on the most recent user visitation survey conducted by the Metropolitan Council; and</p>
          <p>(4) ten percent of the money must be distributed as grants to implementing agencies for land acquisition within Metropolitan Council approved regional parks and trails master plan boundaries under the council's park acquisition opportunity grant program. The Metropolitan Council must provide a match of $2 of the council's park bonds for every $3 of state funds for the park acquisition opportunity grant program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.53.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Data availability.</span></h2>
          <p>Data collected by the projects funded with money from the parks and trails fund that have value for planning and management of natural resources, emergency preparedness, and infrastructure investments must conform to the enterprise information architecture developed by the Department of Information Technology Services. Spatial data must conform to geographic information system guidelines and standards outlined in that architecture and adopted by the Minnesota Geographic Data Clearinghouse at the Minnesota Geospatial Information Office. A description of these data that adheres to the Department of Information Technology Services geographic metadata standards must be submitted to the Minnesota Geospatial Information Office to be made available online through the clearinghouse and the data must be accessible and free to the public unless made private under chapter 13. To the extent practicable, summary data and results of projects and programs funded with money from the parks and trails fund should be readily accessible on the Internet and identified as a parks and trails fund project.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.53.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Restoration evaluations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of natural resources may convene a technical evaluation panel comprised of five members, including one technical representative from the Board of Water and Soil Resources, one technical representative from the Department of Natural Resources, one technical expert from the University of Minnesota or the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities, and two other representatives with expertise related to the project being evaluated. The commissioner may add a technical representative from a unit of federal or local government.</p>
          <p>(b) The members of the technical evaluation panel may not be associated with the restoration, may vary depending upon the projects being reviewed, and shall avoid any potential conflicts of interest.</p>
          <p>(c) Each year, the commissioner may assign a coordinator to identify a sample of up to ten habitat restoration projects completed with parks and trails funding. The coordinator shall secure the restoration plans for the projects specified and direct the technical evaluation panel to evaluate the restorations relative to the law, current science, and the stated goals and standards in the restoration plan and, when applicable, to the Board of Water and Soil Resources' Native Vegetation Establishment and Enhancement Guidelines.</p>
          <p>(d) The coordinator shall summarize the findings of the panel and provide a report to the chairs of the respective house of representatives and senate policy and finance committees with jurisdiction over natural resources and spending from the parks and trails fund. The report shall determine if the restorations are meeting planned goals, any problems with the implementation of restorations, and, if necessary, recommendations on improving restorations. The report shall be focused on improving future restorations.</p>
          <p>(e) Up to one-tenth of one percent of forecasted receipts from the parks and trails fund may be used for restoration evaluations under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.53.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Reserve requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>In any fiscal year, at least five percent of that year's projected tax receipts determined by the most recent forecast for the parks and trails fund must not be appropriated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.53.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Free park days.</span></h2>
          <p>An implementing agency, county, or city that charges an entrance fee or requires a vehicle permit must provide free access to all its parks at least four days each calendar year in order to be eligible for money appropriated from the parks and trails fund. The implementing agency, county, or city must publicly announce the date when entrance will be free at least 30 days in advance of the date it occurs.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.53.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/363/#laws.5.10.0">2008 c 363 art 5 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.107.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 107</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/172/#laws.5.6.0">2009 c 172 art 5 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.1.3.0">2010 c 361 art 1 s 3</a>; art 3 s 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/6/#laws.3.7.0">1Sp2011 c 6 art 3 s 7</a>; art 5 s 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/134/#laws.0.30.0">2013 c 134 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/142/#laws.3.36.0">2013 c 142 art 3 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/2/#laws.5.2.0">1Sp2015 c 2 art 5 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/172/#laws.3.1.0">2016 c 172 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/91/#laws.3.6.0">2017 c 91 art 3 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.2.16.0">2021 c 31 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/40/#laws.3.6.0">2023 c 40 art 3 s 6</a>,7</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.535">
        <h1 class="shn">85.535 PARKS AND TRAILS GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.535.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources shall administer a program to provide grants from the parks and trails fund to support parks and trails of regional or statewide significance. Grants shall not be made under this section for state parks, state recreational areas, or state trails.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.535.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Priorities.</span></h2>
          <p>In awarding trails grants under this section, the commissioner shall give priority to trail projects that provide:</p>
          <p>(1) connectivity;</p>
          <p>(2) enhanced opportunities for commuters; and</p>
          <p>(3) enhanced safety.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.535.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Grant amount.</span></h2>
          <p>A grant amount is not subject to a maximum grant award limitation. Additional consideration shall be given to applicants who provide a nonstate cash match.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.535.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Rule exemption.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner is not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14 in implementing this section, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.535.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/172/#laws.3.9.0">2009 c 172 art 3 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/264/#laws.3.1.0">2012 c 264 art 3 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.85.536">
        <h1 class="shn">85.536 GREATER MINNESOTA REGIONAL PARKS AND TRAILS COMMISSION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.536.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment; purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The Greater Minnesota Regional Parks and Trails Commission is created to undertake system planning and provide recommendations to the legislature for grants funded by the parks and trails fund to counties, cities, and Tribal governments outside of the seven-county metropolitan area for parks and trails of regional significance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.536.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commission.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission shall include 13 members appointed by the governor with two members from each of the regional parks and trails districts determined under subdivision 5 and one member at large. Membership terms, compensation, and removal of members and filling of vacancies are as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0575">15.0575</a>, except that a commission member may be compensated at the rate of up to $125 a day.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.536.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">First appointments.</span></h2>
          <p>The governor shall make the first appointment by June 15, 2013. The governor shall designate six of the first appointees to terms ending on the first Monday in January 2015, and the remainder of the first appointees shall serve terms ending the first Monday in January 2016.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.536.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">First meeting.</span></h2>
          <p>The governor or the governor's designee shall convene the first meeting of the commission by July 15, 2013, and shall act as chair until the commission elects a chair. The commission shall elect a chair at its first meeting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.536.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Districts; plans and hearings.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of natural resources, in consultation with the Greater Minnesota Regional Parks and Trails Coalition, shall establish six regional parks and trails districts in the state encompassing the area outside the seven-county metropolitan area. The commissioner shall establish districts by combining counties and may not assign a county to more than one district.</p>
          <p>(b) The commission shall develop a strategic plan and criteria for determining parks and trails of regional significance that are eligible for funding from the parks and trails fund and meet the criteria under subdivision 6.</p>
          <p>(c) Counties within each district may jointly prepare, after consultation with all affected municipalities, and submit to the commission, and from time to time revise and resubmit to the commission, a master plan for the acquisition and development of parks and trails of regional significance located within the district. Districtwide plans and master plans for individual parks and trails must meet the protocols and criteria as set forth in the greater Minnesota regional parks and trails strategic plan. The counties, after consultation with the commission, shall jointly hold a public hearing on the proposed plan and budget at a time and place determined by the counties. Not less than 15 days before the hearing, the counties shall provide notice of the hearing stating the date, time, and place of the hearing and the place where the proposed plan and budget may be examined by any interested person. At any hearing, interested persons shall be permitted to present their views on the plan and budget.</p>
          <p>(d) The commission shall review each master plan to determine whether it meets the conditions of subdivision 6. If it does not, the commission shall return the plan with its comments to the district for revision and resubmittal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.536.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Regional significance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission must determine whether a park or trail is regionally significant under this section based on the definitions and criteria determined in the greater Minnesota parks and trails strategic plan, along with the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) a park must provide a natural resource-based setting and should provide outdoor recreation facilities and multiple activities that are primarily natural resource-based;</p>
          <p>(2) a trail must serve more than a local population and where feasible connect to existing or planned state or regional parks or trails;</p>
          <p>(3) a park or trail must be utilized by a regional population that may encompass multiple jurisdictions; and</p>
          <p>(4) a park may include or a trail may pass unique natural, historic, or cultural features or characteristics.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.536.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Recommendations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In recommending grants under this section, the commission shall make recommendations consistent with master plans.</p>
          <p>(b) The commission shall determine recommended grant amounts through an adopted merit-based evaluation process that includes the level of local financial support. The evaluation process is not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
          <p>(c) When recommending grants, the commission shall consider balance of the grant benefits across greater Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(d) Grants may be recommended only for parks and trails included in a plan approved by the commission under subdivision 5.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.536.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Chair.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission shall annually elect from among its members a chair and other officers necessary for the performance of its duties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.536.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Meetings.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission shall meet at least twice each year. Commission meetings are subject to chapter 13D.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.536.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission shall submit a report by January 15 each year listing its recommendations under subdivision 7, in priority order, to the chairs and ranking minority members of the committees of the senate and house of representatives with primary jurisdiction over legacy appropriations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.536.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Conflict of interest.</span></h2>
          <p>A member of the commission may not participate in or vote on a decision of the commission relating to an organization in which the member has either a direct or indirect financial interest.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.85.536.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, "commission" means the Greater Minnesota Regional Parks and Trails Commission established under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.85.536.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/137/#laws.3.8.0">2013 c 137 art 3 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/40/#laws.3.8.0">2023 c 40 art 3 s 8</a>,9</p>
      </div>
    </div>
